# Visual verification log (游戏视觉)

Red, 2026-10-03 ~18:3x: "I want every visual verification image you make in the future documented in Figma too."

This file holds two things:
1. **§1 The procedure.** Every visual-chat workflow follows it to add a verification slide to Figma.
2. **§3 The index.** One row per check, with the images, the verdict and the Figma slide once it exists.

The backlog collected on 2026-10-03 covers everything since 2026-10-02: 68 checks and 266 images. The backlog (VL000 cover + VL001–VL068) was placed on 2026-10-03 22:20–23:12 and audited on 2026-10-04: every `img:` slot holds its image (FIT). Later rows are logged by their own workflows.

The 2026-10-07 upkeep pass (after the check that day):
- finished the half-built VL093–094;
- renamed VL86 / VL87 to VL086 / VL087;
- re-fitted VL069–077 to each image's shape;
- placed VL091–092 and VL095–099;
- marked VL078, VL079 and VL085 as images lost;
- updated the cover.

## 1. Procedure: adding a verification slide

### 1.1 What counts

A **verification image** is any image made to answer a visual question, for example:
- before/after pairs;
- contact sheets and review sheets;
- heatmaps, difference images and masks;
- test frames from a harness or from Play mode;
- in-engine and Blender look-dev checks;
- WIP model previews that someone reviews.

**One check = one question with one verdict = one slide.** Several runs of the same question get one slide each only when the verdict changed; otherwise the newest run replaces the older one.

**Not verification images:**
- research and reference media (photos, catalogue scans, trailer or game stills): they go in the research sections;
- raw per-frame captures already summarised by a sheet;
- iCloud conflict copies (`<name> 2.jpg`);
- HUD/vector masters.

### 1.2 Save the images where they last

- **Shot format (Red, 2026-10-22):** every verification image (comparison grids, contact sheets, evidence captures, plates, distance strips) carries the FRONTROOMSSS logo watermark and the fixed header format of `Documentation/SHOT_FORMAT.md`. Make it with `~/FrontRoomsVisualWork/tools/shotframe/shotframe.py` (`wrap` / `grid`, or `S.save(S.frame(...))`); never draw captions by hand. Exempt: art deliverables, Figma boards, decks, marketing media.
- Save every image under `Frontrooms3D/Documentation/research/<area>/images/`. Use JPG q85 at ≤ 1920 px wide for frames and sheets. Use PNG for heatmaps, masks, UI and anything compared pixel for pixel.
- Clones, session scratchpads and `$W` are working space, not an archive: a reboot can wipe a scratchpad (it did on 2026-10-05). If an image only exists there, copy it into the area's `images/` folder before you log it, and log the copy's path.
- The 29 backlog images in 10 rows (R2, F2, F5) that only lived in the old `$SP` were copied to `research/verification_log/media/` on 2026-10-03, so they survived the wipe; §3 points at the copies. The images for VL078, VL079 and VL085 are logged under `research/` paths, but they never reached the main project: they lived only in workflow clones under `$SP`, so the wipe took them.
- Upload limit: 10 MB per image (`upload_assets`). Every backlog image fits; the largest is 7.8 MB.
- An image under 200 px (a HUD sprite) is upscaled by a whole number with nearest-neighbour before upload, and its chip says the factor (`×20`). If it carries alpha, put a dark `backing` rectangle (#2B2921) under its slot so the alpha reads.
- Upload copies (over 4 MB, or longer than 4,096 px, or upscaled sprites) are made in `$W/vlog_upload/` as JPG q90 / PNG; the index keeps the original path.

### 1.3 Pick the images and write the row first

- Choose **1–6 images** that answer the question. Prefer sheets and before|after pairs. Skip raw duplicates.
- Put the most telling image in the large slot.
- **Claim a number before touching Figma.** Re-read this file, then append the row to §3 with the next VL number, `Slide` = `—` and `Figma` = `queued`, and save. Then bump "Next free VL number" in the table above.
- Two workflows never share a number: the number fixes the slide's cell (§1.4), so a clash shows up as an occupied cell. If the cell is taken, stop, re-read this file and take the next number.
- Row fields:

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| VL | `VL<nnn>`, three digits, never reused |
| Date | the images' file time, `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM` (a range if they span more than 2 min) |
| Task | the id from `Documentation/VISUAL_CHAT_TASKS.md` (G10, W1, N1, F2, …) |
| Check | the slide title: ≤ 30 characters, sentence case |
| Verdict | a verdict word from §1.6; a split verdict may name both parts (`PASS B / FAIL A`) |
| Question | the lede: one question, ≤ 100 characters |
| Statement | the bottom line: the answer with its deciding number, ≤ 64 characters, one line |
| Notes | the numbers behind the verdict, and the source report |
| Images | full paths, in slot order |

### 1.4 Where the slide goes

1. **Read the figma-use skill first** (`get_figma_skill skill://figma/figma-use/SKILL.md`), and load `figma-generate-design` before the first write. Pass both in `skillNames`.
2. **Find the section by name**, not by position: `FRONTROOMS · VISUAL VERIFICATION LOG` on page `2099:76`. Other chats sometimes move sections.
3. **If the section does not exist yet, create it** at the proposed origin. Before creating it, do the bounds check:
   - list every SECTION on the page **including nested ones** (absolute bounds);
   - include the agreed spots that are not built yet: ROOM VISUALS (x 42617, y 2000), DOOR BREAK · VISUAL PROPOSAL (x 59977, y 2000) and LEVEL TRANSITIONS. LEVEL TRANSITIONS was agreed at x 33937, y 2000, but TOUCH CONTROLS now stands there, so ask the level-transitions workflow where it went;
   - the new rectangle must clear all of them by ≥ 400 px. If it does not, step right by 8,680 until it does;
   - x 16577 below y 23865 belongs to 平面视觉;
   - then write the section id into the table at the top of this file.
4. **Section size:** width 8,280. Height = rows × 1,240 + 80, where rows = ceil((highest VL number + 1) / 4). Today's height is in the Section row of the table at the top; grow it per this rule.
   - Grow the height when a new row starts.
   - Before growing, re-check the bounds below the section.
   - Write the new height into the Section row in the same pass. The 24-row growth for VL093–094 was never written down, so the table was stale until 2026-10-07.
5. **Slide cell from the number** (4 across, as in the Hunter section). VL000 is the cover and takes the first cell:
   - col = n mod 4, row = floor(n / 4);
   - frame x = 120 + col × 2,040, y = 160 + row × 1,240 (section-relative).
   - Example: VL000 sits at (120, 160), VL001 at (2160, 160) and VL004 at (120, 1400). VL072 starts row 19: grow the section first.
6. **Never edit, move or restyle another chat's section or slide.** Only this section and its slides belong to the visual chat.

### 1.5 Slide grammar (from Hunter 2331:852; numbers in px)

- **Frame:** 1920 × 1080, white fill (#FFFFFF), clip content on, named `VL<nnn> · <task> · <check>` (for example `VL019 · G10 · The milky veil is gone`).
- **Grid:** 72 px side margins, content 1,776 wide. Four columns of 426 with a 24 gutter, at x 72 / 522 / 972 / 1422.
- **Text styles:** the file's P1 styles, applied by id through `setTextStyleIdAsync`. All text is #0A0A0A unless it sits on a chip. **No Inter, no SF Pro, no SushiGo styles.**

| Role | Style (id) | Face | Size / leading | Box |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Running header | P1/Header/Meta (`S:e15151982bb3e6d485ad51045c6714f087f0c2eb,`) | IBM Plex Mono Regular | 13 / 16 | y 28, h 16 |
| Title | P1/Display/Title (`S:4cc59baf3230acd570aeedd2f3163c738d822402,`) | Bayon | 88 / 80, −1 % | x 72, y 139, one line, ends before x 1248 |
| Lede | P1/Text/Body (`S:60138e985390dcae06a002de377d05c5808fc81c,`) | Source Serif 4 | 24 / 26, −1 % | x 1272, w 576, ≤ 2 lines, bottom on y 225 (a 1-line lede sits at y 199) |
| Chip label | P1/Display/Label (`S:44581afa083a5f9b8da214be893def6dc2449af1,`) | Bayon, UPPER | 20 / 20, +3 % | chip padding 10 / 7, at image x + 16, y + 16 |
| Statement | P1/Text/Statement (`S:8ea64620976dfe88c39216b12a4fa8d0d71a53be,`) | Source Serif 4 | 50 / 42, −2 % | x 72, y 969, w 1776, one line |

- **Running header (the only place for meta):**

| x | Text |
|---|---|
| 72 | `Individual Game Project` |
| 522 | `FrontRooms · Verification log` |
| 972 | the meta line `<date> · <task> · <check> · <verdict>`, e.g. `Oct 3, 2026 · G10 · The milky veil is gone · PASS`. One line, ≤ 90 characters, ends before x 1698 |
| 1722 | `VL<nnn>` (in the page-number slot), always three digits: `VL086`, never `VL86`. The frame name uses the same form |

- **Header date:** take it from the index `Date` field, which stays ISO.
  - One day: `Oct 3, 2026`.
  - A range within one month: `Oct 2–3, 2026` (en dash, no spaces, month once).
  - A range across months: `Sep 30–Oct 2, 2026`.
  - Times never go in the header.
  - The cover uses the span of the whole log, for example `Oct 2–4, 2026`.

- **Media band:** y 232–889 (657 high), x 72–1848. The slots are plain rectangles, corner radius 0, named `img:<file stem>` (the file name without its extension; stems are unique in the backlog).
- **Slots take the image's own aspect ratio** (no letterbox, no crop), so a chip always sits on the picture.
  - **Shrink the slot to the fitted image and anchor it top-left.** The slot's width ÷ height equals the image's width ÷ height. Fractional px are fine (for example 562.67 × 316.5); a slot is never a bigger box with the image centred inside it. Check: `abs(w/h − iw/ih) × h` < 0.5 px for every slot.
  - Slides placed on 2026-10-03/04 use whole-pixel slots; errors under 2 px are accepted. The 27 slots off by 2 px or more were re-fitted on 2026-10-07.
  - Pack the images as rows and columns with 24 px gutters, starting at (72, 232). Put the most telling image largest. Keep the logged order unless a reorder clearly avoids a tiny slot. Space left over at the right or bottom of the band stays white.
  - The default boxes in the table below are the space each image may use, not the slot size. Fit the image inside its box, shrink the slot to the fitted image, and anchor it at the box's top-left. VL069–077 kept the full default boxes with the images centred (FIT letterbox), and were re-fitted on 2026-10-07.
  - Helper: `$W/vlog_fix/layout.py` (2026-10-07) packs the images as a tree of rows and columns at their exact aspect ratios and prints the slot rectangles; `$W/vlog_fix/final_layout.json` holds the rectangles used in the 2026-10-07 upkeep pass, and `$W/vlog_fix/fix_round1.json` those of the fix round that followed (VL071, VL093, VL096 and the 27 legacy re-fits), and `$W/vlog_fix2/fix_round2.json` those of fix round 2 (VL017, VL044, VL065, VL068, VL082). The VL075–077 entries in `final_layout.json` are out of date: the K1 fix stage rebuilt those images at 17:09–17:10 and re-fitted their slots, so never restore them from that file. The 2026-10-03 `layout2.py` was lost with the old scratchpad.
  - Use the best layout in which the first image is the largest. The top score from layout.py is not enough on its own. Areas within 1 % count as equal: on an equal grid the first slot may be 1 px shorter than one on the next row (316 against 317).

| Images | Slots (x, y, w × h) |
|---|---|
| 1 | (72, 232, 1776 × 657) |
| 2 | (72, 232, 876 × 657) · (972, 232, 876 × 657) |
| 3 | (72, 232, 876 × 657) · (972, 232, 876 × 316) · (972, 572, 876 × 317) |
| 4 | (72, 232, 876 × 316) · (972, 232, 876 × 316) · (72, 572, 876 × 317) · (972, 572, 876 × 317). For tall strips or portrait shots, use four columns of 426 × 657 instead (HR15) |
| 5 | (72, 232, 876 × 657) · (972, 232, 426 × 316) · (1422, 232, 426 × 316) · (972, 572, 426 × 317) · (1422, 572, 426 × 317) (HR04) |
| 6 | (72 / 522 / 972 / 1422, 232, 426 × 316) · (72, 572, 876 × 317) · (972, 572, 876 × 317) |

- **Scale mode:** `FIT` for sheets, pairs, heatmaps, charts, crops and anything whose edges are evidence. `FILL` only for a single frame whose crop keeps every bit of the evidence. **Never crop the evidence.**
- **Chips:** optional, 1–4 words, at the image's top-left corner (+16, +16), with two exceptions:
  - in-game frames whose HUD prints the level label top-left take the chip bottom-right (−16, −16);
  - BEFORE | AFTER pairs with labels baked in take the view chip bottom-left (+16, −16).
  - No chip on sheets and composites that carry their own labels.
  - Use them for what tells two images apart: `BEFORE`, `AFTER`, `1.5 M`, `RUN 3`, `×1.5`.
  - Dark chip (#0A0A0A, white text) on light images; yellow chip (#F4DF3B, #0A0A0A text) on dark images.
  - When a slot moves or changes size, move its chips with it, keeping the same corner offset.
- **No captions under images and no source footers.** File stems live in the layer names; paths, numbers and sources live in this file.
- **Say it once.** The lede asks the question and the statement answers it with the deciding number. Neither repeats the title.

### 1.6 Verdict words (header and index)

| Word | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `PASS` / `FAIL` | Measured against a stated bar |
| `FAIL→FIXED` | The slide shows the failed state; a later row shows the fix |
| `FLAG` | Passes, but with a problem that someone owns |
| `RISK` | A known gap that can bite gameplay |
| `PARTIAL` | Part of it passes |
| `FINDING` / `BASELINE` | The state documented, with no gate |
| `PENDING` | Images made, no verdict yet |
| `WAIT-RED` | Red decides |
| `APPROVED` / `ACCEPTED` / `PICK …` / `MERGED` / `DONE` | Decisions and outcomes |
| `SUPERSEDED` | A later run replaces it |

When a later run changes the verdict, add a new row and a new slide. Do not rewrite the old slide.

### 1.7 Build, upload, check

1. **Build the frame in one `use_figma` call** at the cell from §1.4 (cloning an earlier slide is fine, but `clone()` puts the copy on the page, not in the section: `appendChild` it to the section, then set its x / y; found 2026-10-15 with VL289–290):
   - header, title, lede, statement and chips;
   - one empty slot rectangle per image, named `img:<stem>`;
   - return the frame id and the slot ids, in slot order.
2. **Upload** the images:
   - load the tool with ToolSearch `select:mcp__6e617164-c524-4dd6-b5b8-2cd2ded137b0__upload_assets`;
   - call it with `fileKey` `0tCbAiVUlrPId3RWd9LRif`, `currentPageId` `2099:76`, `count` = the number of images, `nodeIds` = the slot ids in the same order, and `scaleMode` per §1.5;
   - `upload_assets` takes **one `scaleMode` per call**. A slide that mixes FIT and FILL slots needs two calls, one per mode, each with only its own `nodeIds`. Batch slides by mode, never by position;
   - POST raw bytes (`Content-Type` header). A multipart upload names the layer after the file and can rename an `img:` slot;
   - the upload replaces the slot's fills, so a grey placeholder fill goes away. Still check that each slot holds exactly one IMAGE fill;
   - **POST every returned URL** before you call `upload_assets` again: `curl -sS -X POST -H 'Content-Type: image/jpeg' --data-binary @"<absolute path>" "<url>"` (use `image/png` for PNG);
   - at most 60 URLs per call;
   - one call per slide keeps a failed upload easy to redo; for a backlog, one call per 8 slides (≤ 60 URLs) is fine. Check afterwards that every `img:` slot holds an IMAGE fill.
3. **Check once:**
   - take one `screenshot()` of the frame;
   - confirm every slot holds its image, nothing is cropped that matters, and no text is clipped or overlapping;
   - if anything is wrong, fix it and take one more screenshot, then stop;
   - this check screenshot is a working file, not a verification image: do not log it.
4. **No half-built slides.** A half-built slide has a frame in its cell but a slot with no image: an empty slot, or a grey #D9D9D9 placeholder. Its row still reads `—` / `queued`.
   - A stage that builds a frame must upload into it before the stage ends. If the upload cannot finish, delete the frame, or set the row's `Figma` field to `half-built <date>, frame <id>` so the next pass can find it.
   - Whoever finds a half-built slide finishes it. Keep the frame and slot ids, check that each slot has its image's aspect ratio, upload, and make sure each slot holds exactly one IMAGE fill. Then take a screenshot and record it (§1.8).
   - If the images are gone, follow the lost-images rule in §1.9.
   - Example: VL093–094 (2637:6093, 2637:6108) stood half-built from 2026-10-04 until 2026-10-07.

### 1.8 Record it

- In §3, set `Slide` to the frame's node id (for example `2601:812`) and `Figma` to `placed <YYYY-MM-DD>`.
- If you changed the section's height or created the section, update the table at the top of this file.
- **The cover is yours too.** Whoever places a slide updates the cover VL000 (`2597:6093`) in the same pass:
  - the header counts (`<span> · <n> tasks · <n> checks · <n> images · index`, counting placed slides and their `img:` slots);
  - the legend entry for the slide's task: its VL ranges (placed slides only) and its check count;
  - a new task gets a new entry, in order of its first VL number: a label (P1/Display/Label) plus a one-line or two-line text (P1/Text/Body, 426 wide) on the 4-column grid. The rows run from y 290 and must end above y 889; shrink the row pitch (129 px with five rows) before you let the legend reach the statement.
- In the workflow's own report, give the VL number and node id next to each image it cites.
- **Every visual workflow ends with this procedure.** Its last stage logs each check it verified.

### 1.9 Rows left behind

- **Owner:** the visual chat's verification-log upkeep owns every row left `queued` or half-built by a workflow that stopped, crashed or was wiped. Each pass:
  - re-reads §3;
  - places every queued row whose images exist;
  - finishes every half-built slide (§1.7 step 4).
- **Lost images:** when a row's images exist nowhere (not in `research/`, `$W` or a live clone):
  - set `Figma` to `images lost <date>; to be re-shot by <workflow>`;
  - leave `Slide` at `—` and keep the cell empty;
  - do not count the row on the cover;
  - when the workflow resumes and re-shoots, it saves the images under `research/<area>/images/`, places the slide in the row's own cell and records it as usual. The VL number is never reused.

**Not collected, and why:**
- **Research and reference media.** These are not verification images; they stay in the research sections: `level_transitions/images/rb_*`, `room_visuals/images/run_ref_*` and `run_research_*`, `outlets/images/01_canon_*`, and `interactables/proposal/media/kane_*` and `a24_*`.
- **Raw captures summarised by a sheet:**
  - GD3's per-frame `before_track_*` and `before_game_*` (about 1,000 JPG);
  - the per-view T1 renders in `print_p0` (only the views shown);
  - W1's single `tf_*_before` and `tf_*_after` frames (the `_pair` files carry both);
  - `room_visuals/images/room_*` beyond the sheets;
  - `level_transitions/images/cut_*` beyond the sheet and five representatives;
  - `v_lightlead_shot1–4`, which are byte-identical to `var_lightlead_shot1–4`.
- **K1 masters and composites:** `ui_key_icon/design/hud` (182), `design/png`, `design/svg` and `design/dropin`. 平面视觉 rebuilds the key-HUD variants natively in `2532:4038`; the K1 slides log only the legibility evidence.
- **iCloud conflict copies** (`* 2.jpg`, `* 2.png`) in `glass/destruction/build/images` and `build/masks`.
- **Outside the search scope, picked up when their workflows log them:**
  - **WebGL:** `research/webgl` has no images. The budget view frames were in `$SP/proj_web/Verification/webgl-budget/views` (lost in the wipe; re-shoot under `$W`, then copy to `research/webgl/images/`).
  - **DB1 door break:** `research/door_break/images` is empty while the workflow runs. Blender quick renders were in `$SP/door_break/{A,B,C}/renders` (lost in the wipe; re-render under `$W`, then copy to `research/door_break/images/`).
  - **N2 Run! directions:** renders still pending.
  - **D1 G1–G3 build critic renders:** in the build clones' `out` folders.
  - **F1 and F5 ambient:** Red's screenshots, and the map chat's pixel-diff frames.
  - **Older autopilot and audit runs** in `$SP/audit_run*_frames` (lost in the wipe).

## 3. Index

`Slide` = the Figma frame's node id once placed. `Figma` is one of:
- `queued`;
- `half-built <date>, frame <id>` (§1.7 step 4);
- `images lost <date>; to be re-shot by …` (§1.9);
- `placed <date>`. When a later pass changes a placed slide, add `, re-fit <date>`, `, renamed <date>` or `, updated <date>`. Image paths are in slot order: the first image goes in the large slot.

| VL126 | 2781:6122 | placed 2026-10-07 | 2026-10-07 17:22–17:37 | codex-audit | Zone key turns pink after R | FAIL | Does the zone key keep its material after the player restarts with R? | After R every key draws magenta; a private copy fixes it. | Batch Play Mode, `FrontRooms3D.unity`, seed 516574485, harness `FrontRoomsCxPlayRun -cxRestartAt 12 -cxKeyShotAt 10` (R = the game's own static restart + `LoadScene`), close-up through a copy of the game camera, 1600 × 900. HEAD 279c144 (`W/proj_cx`): at 10 s the nearest key uses `Map test / key` (URP Lit); 1.5 s after R its material is NULL and it draws Unity's magenta (2.37 % of the frame). The same NULL key appears after a natural caught restart (RT-off run, map 29.0 s). Pre-Codex 7320ed1 (`W/proj_cx_base`): the same NULL key after R, so this is not Codex's. Still there on main a5262fb (2026-10-07 19:21, forced R at 100 s: key NULL, 2.37 % magenta, `research/codex_audit/runtime/head_a5262fb/play/head_a5262fb/closeup_key_after_R.jpg`). Introduced in f5e99e10 (2026-10-02). Cause: `FrontRoomsMapWorld.BuildMaterials` wraps `FrontRoomsSurfaces.Lit(...)` (a static cache shared across map instances) in `Own()`, and `Release()` destroys owned materials with a deferred `Destroy` after the new map has taken the cached one. Map contract patch: `keyGlow = Own(new Material(FrontRoomsSurfaces.Lit(...)))`; with it the key keeps its material after R and 0 renderers draw pink (`W/proj_cx_fix`). Patch: `research/codex_audit/runtime/head_279c144/patches/R2_key_material_contract.diff`. The first-life frame is kept beside it, not on the slide: `research/codex_audit/images/key_first_life_head.jpg`. Report: `research/codex_audit/10_review_runtime.md` R2. | `research/codex_audit/images/key_after_r_head.jpg`<br>`research/codex_audit/images/key_after_r_precodex_7320ed1.jpg`<br>`research/codex_audit/images/key_after_r_contract_patch.jpg` |

| VL195 | 2855:6177 | placed 2026-10-08 | 2026-10-08 00:08–01:15 | N2 | Box probes reach the floor | PASS | Does the glossy waxed VCT reflect the corridor through box-projected probes baked per state? | After the fix, probes move 41 % of goal-view px (was 0.04 %). | Red's 2026-10-03 decision: glossy waxed 12 in VCT (smoothness field .80, lane .85, seams .38) with a box-projected probe set per Run room. Two faults found and fixed in the clone: (1) the probe boxes matched the shell exactly (x ±1.42, y 0–2.9, blend .3); URP weights a probe by distance-to-face / blend, so every floor, wall and soffit pixel weighed 0 and fell back to the flat default cube (r4: probes ×0 / ×1 / ×3 changed S1b by ≤ 9 of 765 levels). The boxes now overhang the shell by .16 m (blend .15). (2) in batch, ReflectionProbe enabled / intensity / customBakedTexture changes after the first render never reach URP 17.3's Forward+ probe atlas (cached by instance id; no editor frame passes), so swapping one probe's cube left the armed cube in tripped frames (r5: bluish far walls). Each state now renders in its own Unity run with its own fixed probe set (`-runState armed|tripped|none`). Debug: probes magenta, default cube green: no green pixel. r8 vs probes off: x_goal 846,784 px (40.8 %) change > 6 levels, S1b 10,911 px (0.5 %, the far floor reflects the lit goal). Matte = the Run_ServiceVCT_S mask with one flat cube. Production note: the game's rig must not swap probe textures at runtime without a frame between. Fixes found by this check: gradient first-stop midpoint, frame strokes from a default object style, MacRoman text decoding, 24 pt slack only on Figma auto-width text, echo-S gradients pinned to the 9 pt glyph (a text gradient's start is measured from its frame, not the page; page coordinates made the echoes vanish), chips moved bottom-right because the title fills the top-left, a GREP style that sets the middle dot in Plex Mono (Bayon has no U+00B7). FLAG, one item left: three questions wrap differently (favourite aspects, confusion, magic wand). CoreText on the project's variable Source Serif 4 confirms that Figma sets optical size 10: Figma's breaks need opsz ≤ 10.5, and InDesign has Adobe's static Text cut (opsz 20). The build switches to Source Serif 4 Caption (opsz 8, which gives all the roman breaks) once Red activates it in Adobe Fonts; the italic prompt 'When were you unsure…' may then run about 4 pt long.
